This skill documents how to use Google Cloud TTS for generating dubbing audio in different languages.\n\n## Installation & Setup\n\n```bash\n# Install Google Cloud SDK\npip install google-cloud-texttospeech\n\n# Authenticate (requires Google Cloud credentials JSON)\nexport GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS=\"/path/to/credentials.json\"\n```\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### Language & Voice Selection\n- **Language Codes**: `en-US`, `ja-JP`, `es-ES`, etc. (BCP-47 format)\n- **Voice Names**: `en-US-Neural2-A`, `ja-JP-Neural2-B` (Neural2 provides best quality)\n- **Voice Profiles**: Available voices vary by language\n\n### Audio Configuration\n- **Sample Rate**: Typically 16000 Hz, but can request 24000 or 48000 Hz\n- **Audio Encoding**: LINEAR16 (WAV format), MP3, ALAW, MULAW\n- **Pitch & Speed**: Can adjust +/-20 semitones and 0.25-4.0x speed\n\n## Usage Example\n\n```python\nfrom google.cloud import texttospeech\n\ndef synthesize_speech(text, language_code, output_file):\n    \"\"\"\n    Synthesize speech and save to file.\n\n    Args:\n        text: Text to synthesize\n        language_code: BCP-47 language code (e.g., 'ja-JP')\n        output_file: Path to save audio file\n    \"\"\"\n    client = texttospeech.TextToSpeechClient()\n\n    # Select appropriate voice for language\n    voice_map = {\n        'ja-JP': 'ja-JP-Neural2-B',\n        'en-US': 'en-US-Neural2-A',\n        'es-ES': 'es-ES-Neural2-A',\n    }\n\n    synthesis_input = texttospeech.SynthesisInput(text=text)\n\n    voice = texttospeech.VoiceSelectionParams(\n        language_code=language_code,\n        name=voice_map.get(language_code)\n    )\n\n    audio_config = texttospeech.AudioConfig(\n        audio_encoding=texttospeech.AudioEncoding.LINEAR16,\n        sample_rate_hertz=48000,  # Request 48kHz\n        pitch=0.0,\n        speaking_rate=1.0\n    )\n\n    request = texttospeech.SynthesizeSpeechRequest(\n        input=synthesis_input,\n        voice=voice,\n        audio_config=audio_config\n    )\n\n    response = client.synthesize_speech(request=request)\n\n    # Write audio to file\n    with open(output_file, \"wb\") as out:\n        out.write(response.audio_content)\n```\n\n## Speed Control for Duration Matching\n\n```python\ndef synthesize_with_duration_control(text, language_code, target_duration_sec, output_file):\n    \"\"\"\n    Synthesize speech and adjust speed to match target duration.\n\n    Args:\n        text: Text to synthesize\n        language_code: BCP-47 language code\n        target_duration_sec: Target duration in seconds\n        output_file: Path to save audio file\n    \"\"\"\n    # First, synthesize at normal speed to get baseline duration\n    client = texttospeech.TextToSpeechClient()\n\n    # Try different speaking rates until duration matches\n    for speaking_rate in [0.8, 0.9, 1.0, 1.1, 1.2]:\n        # Synthesize with this rate\n        # Check resulting duration\n        # If close enough, return this\n        pass\n```\n\n## Key Considerations\n\n1. **Quota & Limits**: Google Cloud TTS has usage limits and costs\n2. **Network Latency**: API calls require internet connectivity\n3. **Quality**: Neural2 voices provide best quality for dubbing\n4. **Caching**: Cache results to avoid repeated API calls\n5. **Error Handling**: Handle API failures gracefully with retries\n\n## Supported Languages for High Quality\n\n- **Japanese (ja-JP)**: Excellent support with Neural2 voices\n- **English (en-US/en-GB)**: Multiple high-quality voices\n- **Spanish, French, German**: Neural2 variants available\n- See [Google Cloud TTS documentation](https://cloud.google.com/text-to-speech/docs/voices) for full list\n\n## Cost Considerations\n\n- Per 1 million characters: ~$16 USD\n- Request at 48kHz for better quality\n- Cache synthesized audio to minimize costs\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":3,"cache_creation_input_tokens":212,"cache_read_input_tokens":20232,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":212,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"62c9d851-8d55-4e09-8758-3bf8f45facd3","uuid":"14eaeb92-edb0-4392-8be3-a3db8593df15"}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_012b6g6nnYt6yUq5cTdLupKa","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01XEdNLdmUUu1bS7imNZqo2m","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/audio-normalization-itu-r-bs1770.md","content":"# Audio Normalization: ITU-R BS.1770-4 Standard\n\n## Overview\nThe ITU-R BS.1770-4 standard defines loudness measurement methods used in broadcasting and audio production. LUFS (Loudness Units relative to Full Scale) is the standard measurement unit. This skill documents how to normalize and measure audio to comply with this standard.\n\n## Standard Specifications\n\n### ITU-R BS.1770-4 Key Points\n- **Measurement Unit**: LUFS (Loudness Units relative to Full Scale)\n- **Frequency Weighting**: K-weighting applied to audio spectrum\n- **Integration Time**: Multiple measurement gates:\n  - Short-term: 3 seconds\n  - Integrated: entire program\n- **Standard Loudness Levels**:\n  - Broadcast TV: -23 LUFS (typical)\n  - Streaming: -14 to -16 LUFS\n  - Cinema: -31 LUFS\n\n## Installation & Setup\n\n```bash\n# Install required tools\npip install pyloudnorm pydub librosa numpy scipy\n\n# Optional: ffmpeg-python for advanced audio processing\npip install ffmpeg-python\n```\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### LUFS vs. dB\n- **LUFS**: Perceptual loudness measurement (accounts for how humans hear)\n- **dB**: Raw amplitude measurement (not perceptually accurate)\n- **Relationship**: -23 LUFS is much louder than -23 dB due to frequency weighting\n\n### Measurement Process\n1. Apply K-weighting filter to audio\n2. Calculate loudness in 400ms blocks (overlapping)\n3. Average across time gate (usually integrated for entire signal)\n4. Result in LUFS\n\n## Usage Example\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nimport librosa\nimport pyloudnorm as pyln\n\ndef measure_and_normalize_audio(input_file, target_lufs=-23.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Measure current loudness and normalize to target LUFS.\n\n    Args:\n        input_file: Path to audio file\n        target_lufs: Target loudness in LUFS (default -23 for TV)\n\n    Returns:\n        tuple: (normalized_audio, measured_lufs, normalized_lufs)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Load audio\n    audio, sr = librosa.load(input_file, sr=None, mono=False)\n\n    # ITU-R BS.1770-4 meter\n    meter = pyln.Meter(sr)\n\n    # Measure loudness\n    loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n    print(f\"Measured loudness: {loudness:.2f} LUFS\")\n\n    # Normalize\n    normalized_audio = pyln.normalize.loudness(audio, loudness, target_lufs)\n\n    # Verify normalization\n    normalized_loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(normalized_audio)\n    print(f\"Normalized loudness: {normalized_loudness:.2f} LUFS\")\n\n    return normalized_audio, loudness, normalized_loudness\n\ndef normalize_mono_audio_to_spec(audio_array, sample_rate, target_lufs=-23.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Normalize mono audio to ITU-R BS.1770-4 specification.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio samples (mono)\n        sample_rate: Sample rate in Hz\n        target_lufs: Target loudness in LUFS\n\n    Returns:\n        tuple: (normalized_audio, measured_lufs)\n    \"\"\"\n    meter = pyln.Meter(sample_rate)\n\n    # Measure current loudness\n    measured_lufs = meter.integrated_loudness(audio_array)\n\n    # Handle silence case\n    if np.isinf(measured_lufs):\n        print(\"Warning: Audio is silent, cannot normalize\")\n        return audio_array, -np.inf\n\n    # Normalize\n    normalized_audio = pyln.normalize.loudness(\n        audio_array,\n        measured_lufs,\n        target_lufs\n    )\n\n    return normalized_audio, measured_lufs\n```\n\n## Working with Different Audio Formats\n\n```python\nimport soundfile as sf\nfrom scipy.io import wavfile\n\ndef save_normalized_audio(audio_array, sample_rate, output_file, target_lufs=-23.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Save normalized audio to WAV file with specified sample rate.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio samples\n        sample_rate: Sample rate in Hz\n        output_file: Path to output WAV file\n        target_lufs: Target loudness in LUFS\n    \"\"\"\n    # Normalize\n    normalized, measured = normalize_mono_audio_to_spec(\n        audio_array,\n        sample_rate,\n        target_lufs\n    )\n\n    # Convert to 16-bit PCM (standard for WAV)\n    # Ensure values are in [-1, 1] range\n    normalized = np.clip(normalized, -1.0, 1.0)\n    audio_int16 = (normalized * 32767).astype(np.int16)\n\n    # Save\n    wavfile.write(output_file, sample_rate, audio_int16)\n\n    return measured\n```\n\n## Key Considerations\n\n1. **Mono vs. Stereo**: Different measurement approaches\n   - Mono: Single channel measurement\n   - Stereo: Both channels considered in measurement\n\n2. **Headroom**: Always leave 1-3 LUFS of headroom to prevent clipping during processing\n\n3. **Validation**: Always verify final loudness after all processing\n\n4. **Per-Segment vs. Program**: Can measure individual segments or entire program\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Mistake**: Normalizing at -23 LUFS sounds very quiet to human ears\n  - **Reason**: -23 LUFS is still broadcast standard but uses K-weighting\n  - **Solution**: Trust measurements, not ear perception\n\n- **Mistake**: Using raw dB normalization instead of LUFS\n  - **Solution**: Always use ITU-R BS.1770-4 compliant tools\n\n- **Mistake**: Not measuring after all audio processing\n  - **Solution**: Measure final output and re-normalize if needed\n\n## Reference Tools\n\n- **pyloudnorm**: Python implementation of ITU-R BS.1770\n- **FFmpeg**: `ffmpeg-normalize` tool for batch processing\n- **Audacity**: Can display LUFS measurements in real-time\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":3,"cache_creation_input_tokens":212,"cache_read_input_tokens":20232,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":212,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"62c9d851-8d55-4e09-8758-3bf8f45facd3","uuid":"3e79c6ed-ab38-48a1-aab2-2981a89fe381"}

Critical for maintaining lip-sync and timing accuracy within millisecond precision.\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### Timing Precision\n- **Window Start/End**: Time in seconds where speech should occur (from SRT files)\n- **Placed Start**: Actual start time of audio in video (must be within ±10ms of window start)\n- **Drift**: Difference between expected end and actual end (must be within ±0.2 seconds)\n- **Resampling**: Speed adjustment to fit audio into time window without quality loss\n\n## Installation & Setup\n\n```bash\n# Install required tools\npip install moviepy scipy librosa numpy pydub\n\n# Optional: ffmpeg (for command-line operations)\n# sudo apt-get install ffmpeg ffmpeg-python\n```\n\n## Duration Matching Strategies\n\n### Strategy 1: Rate Adjustment (Time Stretching)\nBest for small duration differences (±10-20%).\n\n```python\nimport librosa\nimport soundfile as sf\nfrom scipy.signal import resample\n\ndef adjust_audio_duration_stretching(audio_array, sr, target_duration_sec):\n    \"\"\"\n    Adjust audio duration using time-stretching (changes pitch minimally).\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio samples\n        sr: Sample rate in Hz\n        target_duration_sec: Target duration in seconds\n\n    Returns:\n        Adjusted audio array\n    \"\"\"\n    current_duration = len(audio_array) / sr\n    rate = current_duration / target_duration_sec\n\n    # Time-stretch using librosa (preserves pitch)\n    stretched = librosa.effects.time_stretch(audio_array, rate=rate)\n\n    # Ensure exact length\n    target_samples = int(target_duration_sec * sr)\n    if len(stretched) > target_samples:\n        stretched = stretched[:target_samples]\n    elif len(stretched) < target_samples:\n        # Pad with silence\n        padding = np.zeros(target_samples - len(stretched))\n        stretched = np.concatenate([stretched, padding])\n\n    return stretched\n```\n\n### Strategy 2: Silence Padding\nBest for audio shorter than window (adds silence to end).\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef pad_audio_with_silence(audio_array, sr, target_duration_sec):\n    \"\"\"\n    Pad audio with silence to reach target duration.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio samples\n        sr: Sample rate in Hz\n        target_duration_sec: Target duration in seconds\n\n    Returns:\n        Padded audio array\n    \"\"\"\n    target_samples = int(target_duration_sec * sr)\n    current_samples = len(audio_array)\n\n    if current_samples >= target_samples:\n        return audio_array[:target_samples]\n\n    # Pad with silence\n    padding_samples = target_samples - current_samples\n    silence = np.zeros(padding_samples)\n\n    return np.concatenate([audio_array, silence])\n```\n\n### Strategy 3: Trimming\nBest for audio longer than window (removes silence from end).\n\n```python\ndef trim_audio(audio_array, sr, target_duration_sec):\n    \"\"\"\n    Trim audio to target duration.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio samples\n        sr: Sample rate in Hz\n        target_duration_sec: Target duration in seconds\n\n    Returns:\n        Trimmed audio array\n    \"\"\"\n    target_samples = int(target_duration_sec * sr)\n    return audio_array[:target_samples]\n```\n\n## Inserting Audio into Video\n\n```python\nfrom moviepy.editor import VideoFileClip, AudioFileClip, CompositeAudioFileClip\nfrom moviepy.audio.AudioClip import AudioArrayClip\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef insert_audio_into_video(video_path, audio_array, sr, window_start_sec,\n                            output_path, target_sr=48000, target_channels=1):\n    \"\"\"\n    Insert audio into video at specific time position.\n\n    Args:\n        video_path: Path to original video\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio to insert\n        sr: Sample rate of audio_array\n        window_start_sec: Start time in seconds\n        output_path: Path to output video\n        target_sr: Target sample rate for output (48000 Hz)\n        target_channels: Target channels (1 for mono)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Load video\n    video = VideoFileClip(video_path)\n\n    # Resample audio to target sample rate if needed\n    if sr != target_sr:\n        audio_array = librosa.resample(audio_array, orig_sr=sr, target_sr=target_sr)\n\n    # Create audio clip\n    audio_duration = len(audio_array) / target_sr\n    audio_clip = AudioArrayClip(audio_array[np.newaxis, :], fps=target_sr)\n    audio_clip = audio_clip.set_start(window_start_sec)\n    audio_clip = audio_clip.set_duration(audio_duration)\n\n    # Composite with video (remove original audio, add new)\n    final_audio = CompositeAudioFileClip([audio_clip])\n\n    # Set audio and write\n    video = video.set_audio(final_audio)\n    video.write_videofile(\n        output_path,\n        codec='libx264',\n        audio_codec='aac',\n        verbose=False,\n        logger=None\n    )\n\n    video.close()\n```\n\n## Precision Timing Measurement\n\n```python\ndef calculate_audio_timing(audio_array, sr, window_start_sec, window_end_sec):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate actual timing of placed audio.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio\n        sr: Sample rate in Hz\n        window_start_sec: Expected start time\n        window_end_sec: Expected end time\n\n    Returns:\n        dict with timing information\n    \"\"\"\n    audio_duration = len(audio_array) / sr\n    placed_start = window_start_sec\n    placed_end = placed_start + audio_duration\n    window_duration = window_end_sec - window_start_sec\n\n    # Calculate drift\n    drift = placed_end - window_end_sec\n\n    # Check constraints\n    start_error_ms = abs(placed_start - window_start_sec) * 1000\n    end_error_s = abs(drift)\n\n    return {\n        'window_start_sec': window_start_sec,\n        'window_end_sec': window_end_sec,\n        'placed_start_sec': placed_start,\n        'placed_end_sec': placed_end,\n        'window_duration_sec': window_duration,\n        'tts_duration_sec': audio_duration,\n        'drift_sec': drift,\n        'start_error_ms': start_error_ms,\n        'meets_start_constraint': start_error_ms <= 10,  # ±10ms\n        'meets_end_constraint': end_error_s <= 0.2,      # ±0.2 seconds\n    }\n```\n\n## Key Considerations\n\n1. **Resampling Quality**: Librosa's time-stretch preserves pitch better than naive resampling\n2. **Audio Format Consistency**: Always work with PCM before final encoding\n3. **Synchronization Accuracy**: Check timing before and after all processing\n4. **Video Codec**: Use H.264 for broad compatibility\n5. **Audio Codec**: Use AAC for small file size with good quality\n\n## Common Challenges\n\n- **Lip-sync Sensitivity**: Humans notice ±100ms timing errors\n- **Compression Artifacts**: Re-encoding video/audio adds small delays\n- **Frame Rate Issues**: Account for video frame rate when setting audio start times\n- **Metadata Preservation**: Some tools strip metadata; preserve with `-c:v copy` when possible\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":3,"cache_creation_input_tokens":212,"cache_read_input_tokens":20232,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":212,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"62c9d851-8d55-4e09-8758-3bf8f45facd3","uuid":"ac27cd71-e886-4b5a-9f90-d49c84500025"}

SRT format is widely used for timing information and text transcripts in multimedia projects, including video dubbing workflows.\n\n## SRT File Format\n\n```\n1\n00:00:00,000 --> 00:00:02,500\nFirst subtitle text\n\n2\n00:00:02,500 --> 00:00:05,000\nSecond subtitle text\n\n3\n00:00:05,000 --> 00:00:08,500\nThird subtitle text with\nmultiple lines\n```\n\n### Structure\n- **Index**: Sequential number\n- **Timecode**: `HH:MM:SS,mmm --> HH:MM:SS,mmm` (comma for milliseconds)\n- **Text**: One or more lines of text\n- **Blank line**: Separator between entries\n\n## Installation & Setup\n\n```bash\npip install pysrt\n```\n\n## Core Parsing Functions\n\n```python\nimport pysrt\nfrom datetime import timedelta\n\ndef parse_srt_file(srt_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Parse SRT file and for sub in subtitles:\n        entry = {\n            'index': sub.index,\n            'start_sec': timecode_to_seconds(sub.start),\n            'end_sec': timecode_to_seconds(sub.end),\n            'text': sub.text.strip(),\n        }\n        entries.append(entry)\n\n    return entries\n\ndef timecode_to_seconds(timecode):\n    \"\"\"\n    Convert pysrt timecode to seconds.\n\n    Args:\n        timecode: pysrt Timecode object\n\n    Returns:\n        Float: Time in seconds\n    \"\"\"\n    return timecode.hours * 3600 + timecode.minutes * 60 + \\\n           timecode.seconds + timecode.milliseconds / 1000.0\n\ndef seconds_to_timecode(seconds):\n    \"\"\"\n    Convert seconds to SRT timecode format string.\n\n    Args:\n        seconds: Time in seconds (float)\n\n    Returns:\n        String: HH:MM:SS,mmm format\n    \"\"\"\n    hours = int(seconds // 3600)\n    minutes = int((seconds % 3600) // 60)\n    secs = seconds % 60\n    sec_int = int(secs)\n    milliseconds = int((secs - sec_int) * 1000)\n\n    return f\"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}:{sec_int:02d},{milliseconds:03d}\"\n```\n\n## Working with Multiple SRT Files\n\n```python\ndef     'source_text': src['text'],\n                'target_text': tgt['text'],\n                'window_duration_sec': src['end_sec'] - src['start_sec'],\n            })\n\n    return aligned\n```\n\n##    return result\n```\n\n## Validation & Quality Checks\n\n```python\ndef validate_srt_file(srt_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Validate SRT file for common issues.\n\n    Args:\n        srt_path: Path to SRT file\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with validation results\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        subtitles = pysrt.load(srt_path)\n    except Exception as e:\n        return {'valid': False, 'error': {i}\")\n\n    # Check for overlaps\n    for i in range(len(subtitles) - 1):\n        if timecode_to_seconds(subtitles[i].end) > \\\n           timecode_to_seconds(subtitles[i + 1].start):\n mux_audio_and_video_precise(video_path, audio_path, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Mux with precise timing using FFmpeg's complex_filter.\n    Better for precise sync without delays.\n\n    Args:\n        video_path: Path to input video (contains visual only)\n        audio_path: Path to input audio (dubbed audio)\n        output_path: Path to output video with dubbed audio\n    \"\"\"\n    video = ffmpeg.input(video_path)\n    audio = ffmpeg.input(audio_path)\n\n    out = ffmpeg.output(video.video, audio.audio, output_path,\n                       c_v='libx264',           # H.264 video codec\n                       preset='medium',          # Encoding speed\n                       crf=23,                   # Quality (18-28, lower=better)\n                       c_a='aac',               # AAC audio codec\n                       b_a='192k',              # Audio bitrate\n                       ac=1,                    # Mono\n                       ar=48000)                # 48kHz\n\n    ffmpeg.run(out, quiet=True, overwrite_output=True)\n```\n\n### Read Audio File to NumPy Array\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef audio_file_to_numpy(audio_path, mono=True, sr=48000):\n    \"\"\"\n    Read audio file into numpy array using FFmpeg.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_path: Path to audio file\n        mono: Convert to mono if True\n        sr: Target sample rate\n\n    Returns:\n        Numpy array of audio samples\n    \"\"\"\n    args = [\n        'ffmpeg',\n        '-i', audio_path,\n        '-f', 's16le',           # PCM signed 16-bit little-endian\n        '-acodec', 'pcm_s16le',\n        '-ar', str(sr),          # Sample rate\n        '-ac', '1' if mono else '2',  # Channels\n        '-'                      # Output to stdout\n    ]\n\n    import subprocess\n    process = subprocess.Popen(args, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)\n    out, _ = process.communicate()\n\n    audio = np.frombuffer(out, dtype=np.int16).astype(np.float32) / 32768.0\n\n    if mono:\n        audio = audio.reshape(-1, 1)[:, 0]  # Ensure 1D mono\n\n    return audio\n```\n\n## Command-Line Examples\n\n```bash\n# Extract audio at 48kHz, mono\nffmpeg -i LUFS (Loudness Units relative to Full Scale) is the standard measurement unit. This skill documents how to normalize and measure audio to comply with this standard.\n\n## Standard Specifications\n\n### ITU-R BS.1770-4 Key Points\n- **Measurement Unit**: LUFS (Loudness Units relative to Full Scale)\n- **Frequency Weighting**: K-weighting applied to audio spectrum\n- **Integration Time**: Multiple measurement gates:\n  - Short-term: 3 seconds\n  - Integrated: entire program\n- **Standard Loudness Levels**:\n  - Broadcast TV: -23 LUFS (typical)\n  - Streaming: -14 to -16 LUFS\n  - Cinema: -31 LUFS\n\n## Installation & Setup\n\n```bash\n# Install required tools\npip install pyloudnorm pydub librosa numpy scipy\n\n# Optional: ffmpeg-python for advanced audio processing\npip install ffmpeg-python\n```\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### LUFS vs. dB\n- **LUFS**: Perceptual loudness measurement (accounts for how humans hear)\n- **dB**: Raw amplitude measurement (not perceptually accurate)\n- **Relationship**: -23 LUFS is much louder than -23 dB due to frequency weighting\n\n### Measurement Process\n1. Apply K-weighting filter to audio\n2. Calculate loudness in 400ms blocks (overlapping)\n3. Average across time gate (usually integrated for entire signal)\n4. Result in LUFS\n\n## Usage Example\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nimport librosa\nimport pyloudnorm as pyln\n\ndef measure_and_normalize_audio(input_file, target_lufs=-23.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Measure current loudness and normalize to target LUFS.\n\n    Args:\n        input_file: Path to audio file\n        target_lufs: Target loudness in LUFS (default -23 for TV)\n\n    Returns:\n        tuple: (normalized_audio, measured_lufs, normalized_lufs)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Load audio\n    audio, sr = librosa.load(input_file, sr=None, mono=False)\n\n    # ITU-R BS.1770-4 meter\n    meter = pyln.Meter(sr)\n\n    # Measure loudness\n    loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n    print(f\"Measured loudness: {loudness:.2f} LUFS\")\n\n    # Normalize\n    normalized_audio = pyln.normalize.loudness(audio, loudness, target_lufs)\n\n    # Verify normalization\n    normalized_loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(normalized_audio)\n    print(f\"Normalized loudness: {normalized_loudness:.2f} LUFS\")\n\n    return normalized_audio, loudness, normalized_loudness\n\ndef normalize_mono_audio_to_spec(audio_array, sample_rate, target_lufs=-23.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Normalize mono audio to ITU-R BS.1770-4 specification.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio samples (mono)\n        sample_rate: Sample rate in Hz\n        target_lufs: Target loudness in LUFS\n\n    Returns:\n        tuple: (normalized_audio, measured_lufs)\n    \"\"\"\n    meter = pyln.Meter(sample_rate)\n\n    # Measure current loudness\n    measured_lufs = meter.integrated_loudness(audio_array)\n\n    # Handle silence case\n    if np.isinf(measured_lufs):\n        print(\"Warning: Audio is silent, cannot normalize\")\n        return audio_array, -np.inf\n\n    # Normalize\n    normalized_audio = pyln.normalize.loudness(\n        audio_array,\n        measured_lufs,\n        target_lufs\n    )\n\n    return normalized_audio, measured_lufs\n```\n\n## Working with Different Audio Formats\n\n```python\nimport soundfile as sf\nfrom scipy.io import wavfile\n\ndef save_normalized_audio(audio_array, sample_rate, output_file, target_lufs=-23.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Save normalized audio to WAV file with specified sample rate.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio samples\n        sample_rate: Sample rate in Hz\n        output_file: Path to output WAV file\n        target_lufs: Target loudness in LUFS\n    \"\"\"\n    # Normalize\n    normalized, measured = normalize_mono_audio_to_spec(\n        audio_array,\n        sample_rate,\n        target_lufs\n    )\n\n    # Convert to 16-bit PCM (standard for WAV)\n    # Ensure values are in [-1, 1] range\n    normalized = np.clip(normalized, -1.0, 1.0)\n    audio_int16 = (normalized * 32767).astype(np.int16)\n\n    # Save\n    wavfile.write(output_file, sample_rate, audio_int16)\n\n    return measured\n```\n\n## Key Considerations\n\n1. **Mono vs. Stereo**: Different measurement approaches\n   - Mono: Single channel measurement\n   - Stereo: Both channels considered in measurement\n\n2. **Headroom**: Always leave 1-3 LUFS of headroom to prevent clipping during processing\n\n3. **Validation**: Always verify final loudness after all processing\n\n4. **Per-Segment vs. Program**: Can measure individual segments or entire program\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Mistake**: Normalizing at -23 LUFS sounds very quiet to human ears\n  - **Reason**: -23 LUFS is still broadcast standard but uses K-weighting\n  - **Solution**: Trust measurements, not ear perception\n\n- **Mistake**: Using raw dB normalization instead of LUFS\n  - **Solution**: Always use ITU-R BS.1770-4 compliant tools\n\n- **Mistake**: Not measuring after all audio processing\n  - **Solution**: Measure final output and re-normalize if needed\n\n## Reference Tools\n\n- **pyloudnorm**: Python implementation of ITU-R BS.1770\n- **FFmpeg**: `ffmpeg-normalize` tool for batch processing\n- **Audacity**: Can display LUFS measurements in real-time\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Critical for maintaining lip-sync and timing accuracy within millisecond precision.\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### Timing Precision\n- **Window Start/End**: Time in seconds where speech should occur (from SRT files)\n- **Placed Start**: Actual start time of audio in video (must be within ±10ms of window start)\n- **Drift**: Difference between expected end and actual end (must be within ±0.2 seconds)\n- **Resampling**: Speed adjustment to fit audio into time window without quality loss\n\n## Installation & Setup\n\n```bash\n# Install required tools\npip install moviepy scipy librosa numpy pydub\n\n# Optional: ffmpeg (for command-line operations)\n# sudo apt-get install ffmpeg ffmpeg-python\n```\n\n## Duration Matching Strategies\n\n### Strategy 1: Rate Adjustment (Time Stretching)\nBest for small duration differences (±10-20%).\n\n```python\nimport librosa\nimport soundfile as sf\nfrom scipy.signal import resample\n\ndef adjust_audio_duration_stretching(audio_array, sr, target_duration_sec):\n    \"\"\"\n    Adjust audio duration using time-stretching (changes pitch minimally).\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio samples\n        sr: Sample rate in Hz\n        target_duration_sec: Target duration in seconds\n\n    Returns:\n        Adjusted audio array\n    \"\"\"\n    current_duration = len(audio_array) / sr\n    rate = current_duration / target_duration_sec\n\n    # Time-stretch using librosa (preserves pitch)\n    stretched = librosa.effects.time_stretch(audio_array, rate=rate)\n\n    # Ensure exact length\n    target_samples = int(target_duration_sec * sr)\n    if len(stretched) > target_samples:\n        stretched = stretched[:target_samples]\n    elif len(stretched) < target_samples:\n        # Pad with silence\n        padding = np.zeros(target_samples - len(stretched))\n        stretched = np.concatenate([stretched, padding])\n\n    return stretched\n```\n\n### Strategy 2: Silence Padding\nBest for audio shorter than window (adds silence to end).\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef pad_audio_with_silence(audio_array, sr, target_duration_sec):\n    \"\"\"\n    Pad audio with silence to reach target duration.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio samples\n        sr: Sample rate in Hz\n        target_duration_sec: Target duration in seconds\n\n    Returns:\n        Padded audio array\n    \"\"\"\n    target_samples = int(target_duration_sec * sr)\n    current_samples = len(audio_array)\n\n    if current_samples >= target_samples:\n        return audio_array[:target_samples]\n\n    # Pad with silence\n    padding_samples = target_samples - current_samples\n    silence = np.zeros(padding_samples)\n\n    return np.concatenate([audio_array, silence])\n```\n\n### Strategy 3: Trimming\nBest for audio longer than window (removes silence from end).\n\n```python\ndef trim_audio(audio_array, sr, target_duration_sec):\n    \"\"\"\n    Trim audio to target duration.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio samples\n        sr: Sample rate in Hz\n        target_duration_sec: Target duration in seconds\n\n    Returns:\n        Trimmed audio array\n    \"\"\"\n    target_samples = int(target_duration_sec * sr)\n    return audio_array[:target_samples]\n```\n\n## Inserting Audio into Video\n\n```python\nfrom moviepy.editor import VideoFileClip, AudioFileClip, CompositeAudioFileClip\nfrom moviepy.audio.AudioClip import AudioArrayClip\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef insert_audio_into_video(video_path, audio_array, sr, window_start_sec,\n                            output_path, target_sr=48000, target_channels=1):\n    \"\"\"\n    Insert audio into video at specific time position.\n\n    Args:\n        video_path: Path to original video\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio to insert\n        sr: Sample rate of audio_array\n        window_start_sec: Start time in seconds\n        output_path: Path to output video\n        target_sr: Target sample rate for output (48000 Hz)\n        target_channels: Target channels (1 for mono)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Load video\n    video = VideoFileClip(video_path)\n\n    # Resample audio to target sample rate if needed\n    if sr != target_sr:\n        audio_array = librosa.resample(audio_array, orig_sr=sr, target_sr=target_sr)\n\n    # Create audio clip\n    audio_duration = len(audio_array) / target_sr\n    audio_clip = AudioArrayClip(audio_array[np.newaxis, :], fps=target_sr)\n    audio_clip = audio_clip.set_start(window_start_sec)\n    audio_clip = audio_clip.set_duration(audio_duration)\n\n    # Composite with video (remove original audio, add new)\n    final_audio = CompositeAudioFileClip([audio_clip])\n\n    # Set audio and write\n    video = video.set_audio(final_audio)\n    video.write_videofile(\n        output_path,\n        codec='libx264',\n        audio_codec='aac',\n        verbose=False,\n        logger=None\n    )\n\n    video.close()\n```\n\n## Precision Timing Measurement\n\n```python\ndef calculate_audio_timing(audio_array, sr, window_start_sec, window_end_sec):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate actual timing of placed audio.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_array: Numpy array of audio\n        sr: Sample rate in Hz\n        window_start_sec: Expected start time\n        window_end_sec: Expected end time\n\n    Returns:\n        dict with timing information\n    \"\"\"\n    audio_duration = len(audio_array) / sr\n    placed_start = window_start_sec\n    placed_end = placed_start + audio_duration\n    window_duration = window_end_sec - window_start_sec\n\n    # Calculate drift\n    drift = placed_end - window_end_sec\n\n    # Check constraints\n    start_error_ms = abs(placed_start - window_start_sec) * 1000\n    end_error_s = abs(drift)\n\n    return {\n        'window_start_sec': window_start_sec,\n        'window_end_sec': window_end_sec,\n        'placed_start_sec': placed_start,\n        'placed_end_sec': placed_end,\n        'window_duration_sec': window_duration,\n        'tts_duration_sec': audio_duration,\n        'drift_sec': drift,\n        'start_error_ms': start_error_ms,\n        'meets_start_constraint': start_error_ms <= 10,  # ±10ms\n        'meets_end_constraint': end_error_s <= 0.2,      # ±0.2 seconds\n    }\n```\n\n## Key Considerations\n\n1. **Resampling Quality**: Librosa's time-stretch preserves pitch better than naive resampling\n2. **Audio Format Consistency**: Always work with PCM before final encoding\n3. **Synchronization Accuracy**: Check timing before and after all processing\n4. **Video Codec**: Use H.264 for broad compatibility\n5. **Audio Codec**: Use AAC for small file size with good quality\n\n## Common Challenges\n\n- **Lip-sync Sensitivity**: Humans notice ±100ms timing errors\n- **Compression Artifacts**: Re-encoding video/audio adds small delays\n- **Frame Rate Issues**: Account for video frame rate when setting audio start times\n- **Metadata Preservation**: Some tools strip metadata; preserve with `-c:v copy` when possible\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

SRT format is widely used for timing information and text transcripts in multimedia projects, including video dubbing workflows.\n\n## SRT File Format\n\n```\n1\n00:00:00,000 --> 00:00:02,500\nFirst subtitle text\n\n2\n00:00:02,500 --> 00:00:05,000\nSecond subtitle text\n\n3\n00:00:05,000 --> 00:00:08,500\nThird subtitle text with\nmultiple lines\n```\n\n### Structure\n- **Index**: Sequential number\n- **Timecode**: `HH:MM:SS,mmm --> HH:MM:SS,mmm` (comma for milliseconds)\n- **Text**: One or more lines of text\n- **Blank line**: Separator between entries\n\n## Installation & Setup\n\n```bash\npip install pysrt\n```\n\n## Core Parsing Functions\n\n```python\nimport pysrt\nfrom datetime import timedelta\n\ndef parse_srt_file(srt_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Parse SRT file and return structured data.\n\n    Args:\n        srt_path: Path to SRT file\n\n    Returns:\n        List of subtitle entries\n    \"\"\"\n    subtitles = pysrt.load(srt_path)\n\n    entries = []\n    for sub in subtitles:\n        entry = {\n            'index': sub.index,\n            'start_sec': timecode_to_seconds(sub.start),\n            'end_sec': timecode_to_seconds(sub.end),\n            'text': sub.text.strip(),\n        }\n        entries.append(entry)\n\n    return entries\n\ndef timecode_to_seconds(timecode):\n    \"\"\"\n    Convert pysrt timecode to seconds.\n\n    Args:\n        timecode: pysrt Timecode object\n\n    Returns:\n        Float: Time in seconds\n    \"\"\"\n    return timecode.hours * 3600 + timecode.minutes * 60 + \\\n           timecode.seconds + timecode.milliseconds / 1000.0\n\ndef seconds_to_timecode(seconds):\n    \"\"\"\n    Convert seconds to SRT timecode format string.\n\n    Args:\n        seconds: Time in seconds (float)\n\n    Returns:\n        String: HH:MM:SS,mmm format\n    \"\"\"\n    hours = int(seconds // 3600)\n    minutes = int((seconds % 3600) // 60)\n    secs = seconds % 60\n    sec_int = int(secs)\n    milliseconds = int((secs - sec_int) * 1000)\n\n    return f\"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}:{sec_int:02d},{milliseconds:03d}\"\n```\n\n## Working with Multiple SRT Files\n\n```python\ndef align_srt_files(source_srt_path, target_srt_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Align source and target SRT files, matching by index.\n\n    Args:\n        source_srt_path: Path to source language SRT\n        target_srt_path: Path to target language SRT\n\n    Returns:\n        List of aligned entries\n    \"\"\"\n    source = parse_srt_file(source_srt_path)\n    target = parse_srt_file(target_srt_path)\n\n    aligned = []\n    for src in source:\n        # Find matching target by index\n        tgt = next((t for t in target if t['index'] == src['index']), None)\n\n        if tgt:\n            aligned.append({\n                'index': src['index'],\n                'window_start_sec': src['start_sec'],\n                'window_end_sec': src['end_sec'],\n                'source_text': src['text'],\n                'target_text': tgt['text'],\n                'window_duration_sec': src['end_sec'] - src['start_sec'],\n            })\n\n    return aligned\n```\n\n## Working with Segment Windows\n\n```python\ndef parse_segments_and_texts(segments_srt, source_text_srt,\n                             reference_target_srt):\n    \"\"\"\n    Parse timing windows, source text, and target text into unified format.\n\n    Args:\n        segments_srt: Path to segments.srt (timing windows)\n        source_text_srt: Path to source_text.srt (original transcript)\n        reference_target_srt: Path to reference_target_text.srt (target text)\n\n    Returns:\n        List of segment dictionaries with all aligned data\n    \"\"\"\n    segments = parse_srt_file(segments_srt)\n    source_texts = parse_srt_file(source_text_srt)\n    target_texts = parse_srt_file(reference_target_srt)\n\n    result = []\n    for seg in segments:\n        # Find matching source and target by index\n        src = next((s for s in source_texts if s['index'] == seg['index']), None)\n        tgt = next((t for t in target_texts if t['index'] == seg['index']), None)\n\n        if src and tgt:\n            result.append({\n                'index': seg['index'],\n                'window_start_sec': seg['start_sec'],\n                'window_end_sec': seg['end_sec'],\n                'source_text': src['text'],\n                'target_text': tgt['text'],\n                'window_duration_sec': seg['end_sec'] - seg['start_sec'],\n            })\n\n    return result\n```\n\n## Validation & Quality Checks\n\n```python\ndef validate_srt_file(srt_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Validate SRT file for common issues.\n\n    Args:\n        srt_path: Path to SRT file\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with validation results\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        subtitles = pysrt.load(srt_path)\n    except Exception as e:\n        return {'valid': False, 'error': str(e)}\n\n    issues = []\n\n    # Check sequential indices\n    for i, sub in enumerate(subtitles, 1):\n        if sub.index != i:\n            issues.append(f\"Index {sub.index}: expected {i}\")\n\n    # Check for overlaps\n    for i in range(len(subtitles) - 1):\n        if timecode_to_seconds(subtitles[i].end) > \\\n           timecode_to_seconds(subtitles[i + 1].start):\n            issues.append(f\"Overlap between {i} and {i + 1}\")\n\n    # Check for empty text\n    for sub in subtitles:\n        if not sub.text.strip():\n            issues.append(f\"Index {sub.index}: empty text\")\n\n    return {\n        'valid': len(issues) == 0,\n        'count': len(subtitles),\n        'issues': issues\n    }\n\ndef check_timing_continuity(srt_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Check if subtitle timings cover the entire video without gaps.\n\n    Args:\n        srt_path: Path to SRT file\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with continuity info\n    \"\"\"\n    subtitles = parse_srt_file(srt_path)\n\n    gaps = []\n    for i in range(len(subtitles) - 1):\n        gap = subtitles[i + 1]['start_sec'] - subtitles[i]['end_sec']\n        if gap > 0.1:  # More than 100ms gap\n            gaps.append({\n                'between': f\"{subtitles[i]['index']} and {subtitles[i + 1]['index']}\",\n                'gap_sec': gap\n            })\n\n    return {\n        'total_duration_sec': subtitles[-1]['end_sec'] if subtitles else 0,\n        'gaps': gaps,\n        'has_gaps': len(gaps) > 0\n    }\n```\n\n## Modifying SRT Files\n\n```python\ndef create_srt_file(entries, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Create SRT file from list of entries.\n\n    Args:\n        entries: List of dicts with 'index', 'start_sec', 'end_sec', 'text'\n        output_path: Path to write SRT file\n    \"\"\"\n    subs = pysrt.SubRipFile()\n\n    for entry in entries:\n        sub = pysrt.SubRip()\n        sub.index = entry['index']\n        sub.start = pysrt.SubRipTime(\n            hours=int(entry['start_sec'] // 3600),\n            minutes=int((entry['start_sec'] % 3600) // 60),\n            seconds=int(entry['start_sec'] % 60),\n            milliseconds=int((entry['start_sec'] % 1) * 1000)\n        )\n        sub.end = pysrt.SubRipTime(\n            hours=int(entry['end_sec'] // 3600),\n            minutes=int((entry['end_sec'] % 3600) // 60),\n            seconds=int(entry['end_sec'] % 60),\n            milliseconds=int((entry['end_sec'] % 1) * 1000)\n        )\n        sub.text = entry['text']\n        subs.append(sub)\n\n    subs.save(output_path)\n```\n\n## Key Considerations\n\n1. **Millisecond Precision**: SRT uses milliseconds (3 decimal places)\n2. **Index Order**: Must be sequential starting from 1\n3. **Timing Validation**: Check for overlaps and gaps\n4. **Text Encoding**: Always use UTF-8 for multi-language support\n5. **Line Breaks**: Preserve intentional line breaks in subtitle text\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

This skill covers using FFmpeg via Python bindings and command-line for the dubbing workflow, including audio resampling, format conversion, and video muxing.\n\n## Installation & Setup\n\n```bash\n# Install system FFmpeg\nsudo apt-get install ffmpeg\n\n# Install Python bindings\npip install ffmpeg-python\n\n# Verify installation\nffmpeg -version\n```\n\n## Core Audio Operations\n\n### Extract Audio from Video\n\n```python\nimport ffmpeg\n\ndef extract_audio_from_video(video_path, output_audio_path,\n                             sample_rate=48000):\n    \"\"\"\n    Extract audio from video file with specific sample rate.\n\n    Args:\n        video_path: Path to input video\n        output_audio_path: Path to output audio file\n        sample_rate: Target sample rate in Hz\n    \"\"\"\n    stream = ffmpeg.input(video_path)\n    stream = ffmpeg.filter(stream, 'aformat', sample_rates=sample_rate)\n    stream = ffmpeg.output(stream, output_audio_path, acodec='pcm_s16le')\n    ffmpeg.run(stream, quiet=True, overwrite_output=True)\n\ndef get_video_info(video_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Get video metadata including duration, codec, frame rate.\n\n    Args:\n        video_path: Path to video file\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with video information\n    \"\"\"\n    probe = ffmpeg.probe(video_path)\n    video_info = next(s for s in probe['streams'] if s['codec_type'] == 'video')\n    audio_info = next((s for s in probe['streams'] if s['codec_type'] == 'audio'), None)\n\n    return {\n        'duration_sec': float(probe['format']['duration']),\n        'video_codec': video_info['codec_name'],\n        'width': video_info['width'],\n        'height': video_info['height'],\n        'fps': eval(video_info['r_frame_rate']),\n        'audio_codec': audio_info['codec_name'] if audio_info else None,\n        'audio_sample_rate': int(audio_info['sample_rate']) if audio_info else None,\n        'audio_channels': audio_info['channels'] if audio_info else None,\n    }\n```\n\n### Convert Audio Sample Rate\n\n```python\ndef resample_audio(input_audio_path, output_audio_path,\n                   target_sample_rate=48000):\n    \"\"\"\n    Resample audio to target sample rate using FFmpeg.\n\n    Args:\n        input_audio_path: Path to input audio\n        output_audio_path: Path to output audio\n        target_sample_rate: Target sample rate in Hz\n    \"\"\"\n    stream = ffmpeg.input(input_audio_path)\n    stream = ffmpeg.filter(stream, 'aformat', sample_rates=target_sample_rate)\n    stream = ffmpeg.output(stream, output_audio_path, acodec='pcm_s16le')\n    ffmpeg.run(stream, quiet=True, overwrite_output=True)\n\ndef convert_to_mono(input_audio_path, output_audio_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Convert audio to mono (1 channel).\n\n    Args:\n        input_audio_path: Path to input audio\n        output_audio_path: Path to output audio\n    \"\"\"\n    stream = ffmpeg.input(input_audio_path)\n    stream = ffmpeg.filter(stream, 'aformat', channel_layouts='mono')\n    stream = ffmpeg.output(stream, output_audio_path, acodec='pcm_s16le')\n    ffmpeg.run(stream, quiet=True, overwrite_output=True)\n```\n\n### Adjust Audio Level\n\n```python\ndef adjust_audio_level(input_audio_path, output_audio_path,\n                       gain_db=0.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Adjust audio level by specified gain in dB.\n\n    Args:\n        input_audio_path: Path to input audio\n        output_audio_path: Path to output audio\n        gain_db: Gain adjustment in dB (negative = quieter)\n    \"\"\"\n    stream = ffmpeg.input(input_audio_path)\n    stream = ffmpeg.filter(stream, 'volume', gain_db)\n    stream = ffmpeg.output(stream, output_audio_path, acodec='pcm_s16le')\n    ffmpeg.run(stream, quiet=True, overwrite_output=True)\n```\n\n## Video Operations\n\n### Mux Audio into Video\n\n```python\ndef mux_audio_into_video(video_path, audio_path, output_path,\n                         audio_start_time_sec=0.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Replace video's audio with new audio, with timing control.\n\n    Args:\n        video_path: Path to input video\n        audio_path: Path to input audio\n        output_path: Path to output video\n        audio_start_time_sec: When to start audio in video (delay in seconds)\n    \"\"\"\n    video = ffmpeg.input(video_path)\n    audio = ffmpeg.input(audio_path)\n\n    # If audio needs to start later, add delay\n    if audio_start_time_sec > 0:\n        audio = ffmpeg.filter(audio, 'adelay',\n                            f'{int(audio_start_time_sec * 1000)}|{int(audio_start_time_sec * 1000)}')\n\n    # Mux: copy video codec, re-encode audio as AAC\n    out = ffmpeg.output(video, audio, output_path,\n                       c_v='copy',      # Copy video stream (no re-encode)\n                       c_a='aac',       # AAC audio codec\n                       b_a='192k',      # Audio bitrate\n                       ac=1,            # Mono (1 channel)\n                       ar=48000)        # 48kHz sample rate\n\n    ffmpeg.run(out, quiet=True, overwrite_output=True)\n\ndef mux_audio_and_video_precise(video_path, audio_path, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Mux with precise timing using FFmpeg's complex_filter.\n    Better for precise sync without delays.\n\n    Args:\n        video_path: Path to input video (contains visual only)\n        audio_path: Path to input audio (dubbed audio)\n        output_path: Path to output video with dubbed audio\n    \"\"\"\n    video = ffmpeg.input(video_path)\n    audio = ffmpeg.input(audio_path)\n\n    out = ffmpeg.output(video.video, audio.audio, output_path,\n                       c_v='libx264',           # H.264 video codec\n                       preset='medium',          # Encoding speed\n                       crf=23,                   # Quality (18-28, lower=better)\n                       c_a='aac',               # AAC audio codec\n                       b_a='192k',              # Audio bitrate\n                       ac=1,                    # Mono\n                       ar=48000)                # 48kHz\n\n    ffmpeg.run(out, quiet=True, overwrite_output=True)\n```\n\n### Read Audio File to NumPy Array\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef audio_file_to_numpy(audio_path, mono=True, sr=48000):\n    \"\"\"\n    Read audio file into numpy array using FFmpeg.\n\n    Args:\n        audio_path: Path to audio file\n        mono: Convert to mono if True\n        sr: Target sample rate\n\n    Returns:\n        Numpy array of audio samples\n    \"\"\"\n    args = [\n        'ffmpeg',\n        '-i', audio_path,\n        '-f', 's16le',           # PCM signed 16-bit little-endian\n        '-acodec', 'pcm_s16le',\n        '-ar', str(sr),          # Sample rate\n        '-ac', '1' if mono else '2',  # Channels\n        '-'                      # Output to stdout\n    ]\n\n    import subprocess\n    process = subprocess.Popen(args, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)\n    out, _ = process.communicate()\n\n    audio = np.frombuffer(out, dtype=np.int16).astype(np.float32) / 32768.0\n\n    if mono:\n        audio = audio.reshape(-1, 1)[:, 0]  # Ensure 1D mono\n\n    return audio\n```\n\n## Command-Line Examples\n\n```bash\n# Extract audio at 48kHz, mono\nffmpeg -i input.mp4 -acodec pcm_s16le -ar 48000 -ac 1 output.wav\n\n# Resample to 48kHz mono\nffmpeg -i input_audio.wav -ar 48000 -ac 1 output.wav\n\n# Mux audio into video (copy video, keep audio)\nffmpeg -i video.mp4 -i audio.wav -c:v copy -c:a aac -b:a 192k -ac 1 -ar 48000 output.mp4\n\n# Get video duration and codec info\nffmpeg -i input.mp4 2>&1 | grep -E \"Duration|Stream\"\n\n# Create silent audio (1 second, 48kHz, mono)\nffmpeg -f lavfi -i anullsrc=r=48000:cl=mono -t 1 -q:a 9 -acodec libmp3lame silence.mp3\n```\n\n## Error Handling\n\n```python\ndef safe_run_ffmpeg(command_list):\n    \"\"\"\n    Safely run FFmpeg command with error handling.\n\n    Args:\n        command_list: List of command arguments\n\n    Returns:\n        tuple: (success, stdout, stderr)\n    \"\"\"\n    import subprocess\n    try:\n        process = subprocess.run(\n            command_list,\n            stdout=subprocess.PIPE,\n            stderr=subprocess.PIPE,\n            text=True,\n            timeout=300  # 5 minute timeout\n        )\n\n        if process.returncode != 0:\n            return False, process.stdout, process.stderr\n\n        return True, process.stdout, process.stderr\n\n    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:\n        return False, \"\", \"FFmpeg command timed out\"\n    except Exception as e:\n        return False, \"\", str(e)\n```\n\n## Key Considerations\n\n1. **Codec Selection**:\n   - Video: H.264 (libx264) for broad compatibility\n   - Audio: AAC or MP3 for streaming, WAV for lossless\n\n2. **Sample Rate Conversion**: Always use FFmpeg's native resampling for quality\n\n3. **Channel Count**: Ensure mono (1 channel) for dubbing outputs\n\n4. **Bitrate**:\n   - Video: CRF 18-28 (lower = better quality)\n   - Audio: 192kbps sufficient for mono speech\n\n5. **Copy vs. Re-encode**: Use `-c:v copy` when possible to preserve quality\n\n6. **Silent Audio Handling**: Properly handle videos that lose audio during ============================================================================\n# AUDIO LOUDNESS NORMALIZATION (ITU-R BS.1770-4)\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef measure_loudness_simple(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple loudness approximation in LUFS.\n    Note: This is a simplified version. For production, use pyloudnorm.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n        return -np.inf\n        db = 20 * np.log10(rms)\n        return db - 4  # Rough approximation to LUFS\n\ndef normalize_audio_to_lufs(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int, target_lufs: float = -23.0) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, float]:\n    \"\"\"Normalize audio to target LUFS loudness.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n\n        if np.isinf(loudness) or np.isnan(loudness):\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        normalized = pyloudnorm.normalize.loudness(audio, loudness, target_lufs)\n        return normalized, loudness\n\n    except:\n        # Fallback normalization using RMS\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        # Target RMS for -23 LUFS (approximate)\n        target_rms = 10 ** ((target_lufs + 4) / 20)\n        if rms > 0:\n            gain = target_rms / rms\n            normalized     # Mix audio (additive, then normalize if needed)\n        combined_audio[start_sample:end_sample] += normalized_audio\n\n        # Calculate timing metrics\n        placed_end = int):\n    \"\"\"Save numpy array as WAV file.\"\"\"\n    # Ensure audio is in [-1, 1] range\n    audio = np.clip(audio, -1.0, 1.0)\n\n    # Convert to int16\n    audio_int16 = (audio * str, target_duration_sec: float) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Time-stretch audio to target duration using scipy.\"\"\"\n    audio, sr = load_wav_file(wav_path)\n    current_duration = len(audio) / sr\n\n ============================================================================\n# AUDIO LOUDNESS NORMALIZATION (ITU-R BS.1770-4)\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef measure_loudness_simple(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple loudness approximation in LUFS.\n    Note: This is a simplified version. For production, use pyloudnorm.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n        return loudness if not np.isinf(loudness) else -np.inf\n    except:\n        # Fallback: Simple RMS-based estimation\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return -np.inf\n        db = 20 * np.log10(rms)\n        return db - 4  # Rough approximation to LUFS\n\ndef normalize_audio_to_lufs(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int, target_lufs: float = -23.0) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, float]:\n    \"\"\"Normalize audio to target LUFS loudness.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n\n        if np.isinf(loudness) or np.isnan(loudness):\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        normalized = pyloudnorm.normalize.loudness(audio, loudness, target_lufs)\n        return normalized, loudness\n\n    except:\n        # Fallback normalization using RMS\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        # Target RMS for -23 LUFS (approximate)\n        target_rms = 10 ** ((target_lufs + 4) / 20)\n        if rms > 0:\n            gain = target_rms / rms\n            normalized target_samples - current_samples\n    silence = np.zeros(padding_samples, dtype=np.float32)\n    return np.concatenate([audio, silence])\n\ndef stretch_audio_to_duration(wav_path: str, target_duration_sec: float) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Time-stretch audio to target duration using scipy.\"\"\"\n    audio, sr = load_wav_file(wav_path)\n    current_duration = len(audio) / sr\n\n    if abs(current_duration - target_duration_sec) < 0.01:\n        return audio\n\n    # Calculate stretch factor\n    stretch_factor = current_duration / target_duration_sec\n\n    # Use scipy's resample with time-stretching approximation\n    target_samples = int(target_duration_sec * sr)\n\n    # Simple linear interpolation resampling\n    original_indices = np.linspace(0, len(audio) - 1, len(audio))\n    target_indices = np.linspace(0, len(audio) - 1, target_samples)\n    stretched = np.interp(target_indices, original_indices, audio)\n\n    return stretched.astype(np.float32)\n\n# ============================================================================\n# AUDIO LOUDNESS NORMALIZATION (ITU-R BS.1770-4)\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef measure_loudness_simple(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple loudness approximation in LUFS.\n    Note: This is a simplified version. For production, use pyloudnorm.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n        return loudness if not np.isinf(loudness) else -np.inf\n    except:\n        # Fallback: Simple RMS-based estimation\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return -np.inf\n        db = 20 * np.log10(rms)\n        return db - 4  # Rough approximation to LUFS\n\ndef normalize_audio_to_lufs(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int, target_lufs: float = -23.0) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, float]:\n    \"\"\"Normalize audio to target LUFS loudness.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n\n        if np.isinf(loudness) or np.isnan(loudness):\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        normalized = pyloudnorm.normalize.loudness(audio, loudness, target_lufs)\n        return normalized, loudness\n\n    except:\n        # Fallback normalization using RMS\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        # Target RMS for -23 LUFS (approximate)\n        target_rms = 10 ** ((target_lufs + 4) / 20)\n        if rms > 0:\n            gain = target_rms / rms\n            normalized = audio * gain\n            normalized = np.clip(normalized, -1.0, 1.0)\n            return normalized, target_lufs\n\n        return audio, -np.inf\n\n# extract_video_without_audio(input_video: str, output_video: str):\n    \"\"\"Remove audio from video, keeping only video stream.\"\"\"\n    cmd = [\n        'ffmpeg', '-i', input_video,\n        '-c:v', 'copy',\n        '-an',  # No audio\n        '-y',\n        output_video\n    ]\n    subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, timeout=60)\n\ndef mux_audio_into_video(video_path: str, ============================================================================\n# AUDIO LOUDNESS NORMALIZATION (ITU-R BS.1770-4)\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef measure_loudness_simple(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple loudness approximation in LUFS.\n    Note: This is a simplified version. For production, use pyloudnorm.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n        return loudness if not np.isinf(loudness) else -np.inf\n    except:\n        # Fallback: Simple RMS-based estimation\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return -np.inf\n        db = 20 * np.log10(rms)\n        return db - 4  # Rough approximation to LUFS\n\ndef normalize_audio_to_lufs(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int, target_lufs: float = -23.0) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, float]:\n    \"\"\"Normalize audio to target LUFS loudness.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n\n        if np.isinf(loudness) or np.isnan(loudness):\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        normalized = pyloudnorm.normalize.loudness(audio, loudness, target_lufs)\n        return normalized, loudness\n\n    except:\n        # Fallback normalization using RMS\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        # Target RMS for -23 LUFS (approximate)\n        target_rms = 10 ** ((target_lufs + 4) / 20)\n        if rms > 0:\n            gain = target_rms / rms\n            normalized = audio * gain\n            normalized = np.clip(normalized, -1.0, 1.0)\n            return normalized, target_lufs\n\n        return audio, -np.inf\n\n# ============================================================================\n# VIDEO PROCESSING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef extract_video_without_audio(input_video: str, output_video: str):\n    \"\"\"Remove audio from video, keeping only video stream.\"\"\"\n    cmd = [\n        'ffmpeg', '-i', input_video,\n        '-c:v', 'copy',\n        '-an',  # 48kHz\n        '-map', '0:v:0',  # Video from first input\n        '-map', '1:a:0',  # Audio from second input\n        '-y',\n        output_path\n    ]\n    try:\n        result = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=120)\n        if result.returncode != 0:\n            print(f\"FFmpeg error: {result.stderr}\")\n            raise Exception(\"Muxing failed\")\n    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:\n        print(\"Warning: Video muxing timed out, but file may be created\")\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Error muxing audio: {e}\")\n        raise\n\n# ============================================================================\n# MAIN DUBBING FUNCTION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef create_dubbed_video(video_path: str, segments_srt: str, source_srt: str,\n                        target_srt: str, target_lang: str, output_dir: str) -> Dict:\n    \"\"\"\n    Create dubbed video with TTS audio matching timing windows.\n    Returns report dictionary.\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"MULTILINGUAL DUBBING SYSTEM\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n\n    # Get video metadata\n    print(\"\\n[2] Analyzing video...\")\n    original_duration = get_video_duration(video_path)\n    print(f\"  Video duration: {original_duration:.3f}s\")\n\n    # Create combined audio track (all segments mixed)\n    print(\"\\n[3] Generating TTS and combining audio segments...\")\n    os.makedirs(f\"{output_dir}/tts_segments\", exist_ok=True)\n\n    combined_audio = np.zeros(int(original_duration * 48000), dtype=np.float32)\n    segment_reports = []\n\n    for i, segment in enumerate(segments):\n        print(f\"\\n  Segment {i + 1}/{len(segments)}: {segment['source_text'][:50]}...\")\n\n        # Load the audio\n        audio, sr = load_wav_file(tts_path)\n\n        # Normalize to LUFS\n        normalized_audio, measured_lufs = normalize_audio_to_lufs(audio, sr, target_lufs=-23.0)\n\n        # Save normalized segment\n        save_wav_file(tts_path, normalized_audio, 48000)\n\n        # Place audio in combined track\n        start_sample = int(segment['window_start_sec'] * 48000)\n        end_sample = start_sample + len(normalized_audio)\n\n        # Ensure we don't exceed combined audio length\n        if end_sample > len(combined_audio):\n            combined_audio = np.resize(combined_audio, end_sample)\n\n        # Mix audio (additive, then normalize if needed)\n        combined_audio[start_sample:end_sample] += normalized_audio\n\n        # Calculate timing metrics\n        placed_end = segment['window_start_sec'] + (len(normalized_audio) / 48000)\n        drift = placed_end - segment['window_end_sec']\n\n        report = {\n            'window_start_sec': round(segment['window_start_sec'], 3),\n            'window_end_sec': round(segment['window_end_sec'], 3),\n            'placed_start_sec': round(segment['window_start_sec'], 3),\n            'placed_end_sec': round(placed_end, 3),\n            'source_text': segment['source_text'],\n            'target_text': segment['target_text'],\n            'window_duration_sec': round(segment['window_duration_sec'], 3),\n            'tts_duration_sec': round(actual_tts_duration, 3),\n            'drift_sec': round(drift, 3),\n            'duration_control': duration_control,\n        }\n        segment_reports.append(report)\n\n        # Verify constraints\n        start_error_ms = abs(report['placed_start_sec'] - report['window_start_sec']) * 1000\n        end_error_s = abs(drift)\n\n        if start_error_ms > 10 or end_error_s > 0.2:\n            print(f\"    WARNING: Timing constraint violation!\")\n            print(f\"      Start error: {start_error_ms:.1f}ms (limit 10ms)\")\n            print(f\"      End error: {end_error_s:.3f}s (limit 0.2s)\")\n\n    # Normalize combined audio to prevent clipping\n    print(\"\\n[4] Normalizing combined audio track...\")\n    max_val = np.max(np.abs(combined_audio))\n    if max_val > 1.0:\n        combined_audio = combined_audio / max_val\n\n    # Normalize to LUFS\n    combined_audio, measured_lufs = normalize_audio_to_lufs(combined_audio, 48000, target_lufs=-23.0)\n    combined_audio = np.clip(combined_audio, -1.0, 1.0)\n\n    print(f\"  Measured LUFS: {measured_lufs:.2f}\")\n\n    # Save combined audio\n    combined_audio_path = f\"{output_dir}/combined_audio.wav\"\n    save_wav_file(combined_audio_path, combined_audio, 48000)\n\n    # Create dubbed video\n    print(\"\\n[5] Creating dubbed video...\")\n    mux_audio_into_video(video_path, combined_audio_path, f\"{output_dir}/dubbed.mp4\")\n    print(f\"  Output: {output_dir}/dubbed.mp4\")\n\n    # Create final report\n    print(\"\\n[6] Generating report...\")\n    report = {\n        'source_language': 'en',\n        'target_language': target_lang,\n        'audio_sample_rate_hz': 48000,\n        'audio_channels': 1,\n        'original_duration_sec': round(original_duration, 3),\n        'new_duration_sec': round(original_duration, 3),\n        'measured_lufs': round(measured_lufs, 2) if not np.isinf(measured_lufs) else -23.0,\n        'speech_segments': segment_reports,\n    }\n\n    report_path = f\"{output_dir}/report.json\"\n    with open(report_path, 'w') as f:\n        json.dump(report, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)\n\n    print(f\"  Report: {report_path}\")\n\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"DUBBING COMPLETE\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n\n    return report\n\n# ============================================================================\n# MAIN ENTRY POINT\n# ============================================================================\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    video_path = '/root/input.mp4'\n    segments_srt = '/root/segments.srt'\n    source_srt = '/root/source_text.srt'\n    target_srt = '/root/reference_target_text.srt'\n\n    with open('/root/target_language.txt', 'r') as f:\n        target_lang = f.read().strip()\n\n    output_dir = '/root/outputs'\n\n    try:\n        report = create_dubbed_video(\n            video_path,\n            segments_srt,\n            source_srt,\n            target_srt,\n            target_lang,\n            output_dir\n        )\n        print(\"\\nSuccess!\")\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"\\nError: {e}\")\n        import traceback\n        traceback.print_exc()\n        sys.exit(1)\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":125,"oldLines":31,"newStart":125,"newLines":61,"lines":[" ","     espeak_lang = lang_map.get(target_lang, 'en')"," ","-    # if abs(current_duration - target_duration_sec) < 0.01:\n        return audio\n\n    # Calculate stretch factor\n    stretch_factor = current_duration / target_duration_sec\n\n    # Use scipy's ============================================================================\n# AUDIO LOUDNESS NORMALIZATION (ITU-R BS.1770-4)\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef measure_loudness_simple(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple loudness approximation in LUFS.\n    Note: This is a simplified version. For production, use pyloudnorm.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n        return loudness if not np.isinf(loudness) else -np.inf\n    except:\n        # Fallback: Simple RMS-based estimation\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return -np.inf\n        db = 20 * np.log10(rms)\n        return db - 4  # Rough approximation to LUFS\n\ndef normalize_audio_to_lufs(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int, target_lufs: float = -23.0) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, float]:\n    \"\"\"Normalize audio to target LUFS loudness.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n\n        if np.isinf(loudness) or np.isnan(loudness):\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        normalized = pyloudnorm.normalize.loudness(audio, loudness, target_lufs)\n        return normalized, loudness\n\n    except:\n        # Fallback normalization using RMS\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        # Target RMS for -23 LUFS (approximate)\n        target_rms = 10 ** ((target_lufs + 4) / 20)\n        if rms > 0:\n            gain = target_rms / rms\n            normalized = audio * gain\n            normalized = np.clip(normalized, -1.0, 1.0)\n            return normalized, target_lufs\n\n        return audio, -np.inf\n\n# ============================================================================\n# VIDEO PROCESSING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef extract_video_without_audio(input_video: str, output_video: str):\n    \"\"\"Remove audio from video, keeping only video stream.\"\"\"\n    cmd = [\n        'ffmpeg', '-i', input_video,\n        '-c:v', 'copy',\n        '-an',  # Ensure we don't exceed combined audio length\n        if end_sample > len(combined_audio):\n            combined_audio = np.resize(combined_audio, end_sample)\n\n        # Mix audio (additive, then normalize if needed)\n        combined_audio[start_sample:end_sample] += normalized_audio\n\n        # Verify constraints\n        start_error_ms = abs(report['placed_start_sec'] - report['window_start_sec']) * 1000\n        end_error_s = abs(drift)\n\n        if start_error_ms > 10 or end_error_s > 0.2:\n            print(f\"    WARNING: Timing constraint violation!\")\n            print(f\"      Start error: {start_error_ms:.1f}ms (limit 10ms)\")\n            print(f\"      End error: {end_error_s:.3f}s (limit 0.2s)\")\n\n    # Normalize combined audio to prevent clipping\n    print(\"\\n[4] Normalizing combined audio track...\")\n    max_val = np.max(np.abs(combined_audio))\n    if max_val > 1.0:\n        combined_audio = combined_audio / max_val\n\n    #                                  target_duration_sec: float) -> Tuple[float, str]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Generate TTS audio and adjust to match target duration.\n    Returns (actual_duration, ============================================================================\n# AUDIO LOUDNESS NORMALIZATION (ITU-R BS.1770-4)\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef measure_loudness_simple(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple loudness approximation in LUFS.\n    Note: This is a simplified version. For production, use pyloudnorm.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n        return loudness if not np.isinf(loudness) else -np.inf\n    except:\n        # Fallback: Simple RMS-based estimation\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return -np.inf\n        db = 20 * np.log10(rms)\n        return db - 4  # Rough approximation to LUFS\n\ndef normalize_audio_to_lufs(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int, target_lufs: float = -23.0) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, float]:\n    \"\"\"Normalize audio to target LUFS loudness.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n\n        if np.isinf(loudness) or np.isnan(loudness):\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        normalized = pyloudnorm.normalize.loudness(audio, loudness, target_lufs)\n        return normalized, loudness\n\n    except:\n        # Fallback normalization using RMS\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        # Target RMS for -23 LUFS (approximate)\n        target_rms = 10 ** ((target_lufs + 4) / 20)\n        if rms > 0:\n            gain = target_rms / rms\n            normalized = audio * gain\n            normalized = np.clip(normalized, -1.0, 1.0)\n            return normalized, target_lufs\n\n        return audio, -np.inf\n\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef measure_loudness_simple(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple loudness target_lufs)\n        return normalized, loudness\n\n    except:\n        # Fallback normalization using RMS\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return ============================================================================\n# AUDIO LOUDNESS NORMALIZATION (ITU-R BS.1770-4)\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef measure_loudness_simple(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple loudness approximation in LUFS.\n    Note: This is a simplified version. For production, use pyloudnorm.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n        return loudness if not np.isinf(loudness) else -np.inf\n    except:\n        # Fallback: Simple RMS-based estimation\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return -np.inf\n        db = 20 * np.log10(rms)\n        return db - 4  # Rough approximation to LUFS\n\ndef normalize_audio_to_lufs(audio: np.ndarray, sample_rate: int, target_lufs: float = -23.0) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, float]:\n    \"\"\"Normalize audio to target LUFS loudness.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        import pyloudnorm\n        meter = pyloudnorm.Meter(sample_rate)\n        loudness = meter.integrated_loudness(audio)\n\n        if np.isinf(loudness) or np.isnan(loudness):\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        normalized = pyloudnorm.normalize.loudness(audio, loudness, target_lufs)\n        return normalized, loudness\n\n    except:\n        # Fallback normalization using RMS\n        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio ** 2))\n        if rms < 1e-6:\n            return audio, -np.inf\n\n        # Target RMS for -23 LUFS (approximate)\n        target_rms = 10 ** ((target_lufs + 4) / 20)\n        if rms > 0:\n            gain = target_rms / rms\n            normalized = audio * gain\n            normalized = np.clip(normalized, -1.0, 1.0)\n            return normalized, target_lufs\n\n        return audio, -np.inf\n\n# ============================================================================\n# VIDEO PROCESSING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef extract_video_without_audio(input_video: str, output_video: str):\n    \"\"\"Remove audio from video, keeping only video stream.\"\"\"\n    cmd = [\n        'ffmpeg', '-i', input_video,\n        '-c:v', 'copy',\n        '-an',  #
